> **QS2S.** Below is a passage written by seat Y, and the thing it responds to, written or done by a DIFFERENT seat X (a pane, the
> librarian or the chair). **In Y's passage, is Y correcting something X said or did?** Correcting means: stating that a claim of X's was
> wrong or overstated; finding a defect in X's work (a red, a failed check, a bug owned by X); or overriding, reversing or refusing a
> choice, ruling, premise or framing X made. **Not a correction by itself:** carrying out X's request; approving it (GREEN, "agree"); a
> question; a new finding that contradicts nothing X said; a mechanical gate's refusal (a hook is not a seat); Y correcting ITSELF.
> Answer: **YES** · **NO** · **CAN'T TELL**

**The design choice, stated first because it is the one that matters:** K and O come from **one series**, *The

## 2 · The installer rematch fix (`install.ps1` +18/−2): RULING — it cannot disturb a hook it does not already manage

**The change, read line by line (`git diff 89f83758 9649ff6 -- dev/shell/install.ps1`):**
- In the merge loop, while walking every group, a hook matching the entry (`Test-SameHook`) is now treated two ways:
  - **it is dropped from its group** if the entry HAS a `Matcher` AND the group is not that matcher's slot (`ReferenceEquals($g, $slot)`). This prints `REMATCH … was matcher / now matcher` and counts as a change. `$found` stays false, so the existing add path puts it into the slot.
  - **every other hook** in the group goes into `$keep` and is written back. The group is rewritten only if something was dropped.

**The four properties:**
- **Moved, not duplicated:** a hook found in the slot AND elsewhere keeps the slot copy (`$found = $true`) and loses the stray one.
- **Unmatched entries are never moved:** the guard is `$e.Matcher -and -not $inSlot`. On today's manifest only three entries carry a Matcher: second-reader, sources-gate and dispatch-gate. Every SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, Stop, SessionEnd and PreCompact entry is matcher-less, so this path is inert for them.
- **Foreign hooks stay put:** only a hook passing `Test-SameHook` for that entry is dropped; `$keep` preserves the rest.
- **Idempotent:** on a re-run the hook is in its slot, `$inSlot` is true, nothing is dropped, a

# D182 · runs, corners and transitions in the corpus (pane E) · 2026-09-28, night

**Packet:** the chair's D182 brief addition, before A builds `vocab.js`. A reader word is a 28–48 m token, so it is
not a piece. Add runs (length, heading, climb, N) and transitions (counts and P) to `corpus.json` and FINDINGS §7f,
with a test. **Nothing is committed.**

## 0 · The answer first

- **A run of one class is mostly ONE word** (median 1 word per run in every class). The classes change as a corner
  opens and closes, so a same-class run is a PART of a corner, not a whole one.
- **So there is a third aggregate, CORNERS:** consecutive curved words (sweep, turn, tight) turning the same way,
  keyed by the tightest class reached. **This is what a user's "turn" piece should span:**

| a corner reaching | N | words | length m | heading ° |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sweep | 257 | 1 (1–1) | 24 (12–78) | 1.2 (0.6–4.3) |
| turn | 116 | 3 (1–6.5) | 100 (24–482) | 12 (3–62) |
| **tight** | **383** | **5 (2–10)** | **212 (64–618)** | **88 (32–256)** |

- **Against one word:** a tight word is 48 m and 30°, a whole tight corner 212 m and 88°.
- **The grammar, measured (P):**
  - straight → sweep 0.78;
  - sweep → straight 0.49 or turn 0.44;
  - turn → tight 0.46 or sweep 0.43;
  - tight → turn 0.76.
  - Straight → tight is 0.03: FINDINGS §7's "never straight into tight", as a number.
  - Every jump is followed by a straight (19/19): the landing ramp.

**The full run table, the corner table and the 7×7 transition table** (with each row's N) are in FINDINGS §7f
("RUNS, CORNERS AND TRANSITIONS") and in `corpus.json` (`runs`, `corners`, `transitions`).

## 1 · How a run is delimited, and why

**Exactly the same class and the same turn direction. No tolerance for a one-token interruption.**
- **No tolerance, because** the reader already folds any run under 12 m into its neighbour. A second tolerance would
  absorb real short pieces and make a run depend on a tuning parameter.
- **The same direction, because** a builder piece turns one way: a left into a right is two pieces (an S).
- **This covers wall-rides and inversions too:** the reader names them by the turn under them (e.g.
  `bowl-turnL^wall`), which is why wall-ride → wall-ride (0.55) and inversion → inversion (0.70) are real direction
  reversals. Only a straight has no direction, and cannot follow itself.
- **A skipped (shared-road) word breaks a run,** and no transition is counted across it. The lap is not wrapped from
  its

# D205 A — the install.ps1 merge fix (B's finding, p-d203-B §3) — hand-back
Worktree `C:\Users\nname\Desktop\a-d205-wt` (a `git worktree add --detach` of the lighthouse repo at 2373a3f5; no junctions; clean after the commit). Sonnet 5.5. HOOK tier. Nothing pushed; the shared checkout untouched. **This lap does not change second-reader behaviour** (the installed hook and worker are byte-identical to before: sha256 `70ab46267e462d60`, `ee7c1e0d6664656e`; `consonance/hooks/` is untouched by this commit).

**Commit (in a-d205-wt only): `ace5fd6b98f04fb24dd99236a65919dcbdf08dda`**, parent 2373a3f5. Two files:
- `dev/shell/install.ps1` — sha256 (committed blob, first 16) **`f360f0e5f4ab313a`**
- `consonance/tools/install-only.test.js` — **`8f98b5b02a3e63fb`**
(Command: `git show ace5fd6:<path> | sha256sum`.)

## What was wrong (B's finding, re-read at the code before changing it) and the fix
1. **The placeholder.** The merge seeded `[pscustomobject]@{ hooks = @() }` for EVERY event array that was new; only an UNMATCHED entry uses it, so the first-ever entry on an event that carried a `Matcher` left it behind: the stray `{ "hooks": [] }` in `~/.claude/settings.json` PreToolUse. **Fix:** seed it only `if ($groups.Count -eq 0 -and -not $e.Matcher)`.
2. **Unmatched → "the last group".** On PreToolUse the last group is now the second reader's `call_librarian|call_chair` group, so a future unmatched PreToolUse hook would have been silently scoped to those two tools instead of firing on every tool. **Fix:** an unmatched entry takes the LAST group that has NO matcher (so on any event whose groups are all matcher-less the behaviour is exactly what it was), and a new matcher-less group is made when there is none.
3. **(B's optional item, done, with limits.)** Empty-hooks groups are pruned **only on events this run registered for** (non-excluded entries), **never a group that holds any hook** (a hook the script did not install stays), and a prune **counts as a change** (the write condition gained `-and $pruned -eq 0`). Without that last part a run that changes nothing else would say "already correct" and never remove the stray group that is on D now. The run prints `PRUNE <event> N empty group(s) removed`, and the summary line gained "N empty group(s) pruned".

## Tests (heavy-run lock, `--test-concurrency=1`; no full suite)
- `consonance/tools/install-only.test.js` (the file's own sandbox: a throwaway repo copy and a throwaway USERPROFILE): **32/32** (was 17; 15 added, 8

Mutant 1 is therefore run with the prune switched off as well, which shows fix 1 stands on its own. The two older mutants of the file (`-Only` filter, the `Excluded` guard) still pass.

## The deny contract — what I read, and what it does and does not say
Source: `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks`, fetched 2026-10-02 (a fetch that summarises the page, so the quoted lines are as returned, not a byte copy). From it: PreToolUse input has `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`. **Deny:** exit 0 with `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"..."}}`; "exit 2 unconditionally blocks"; **"The `permissionDecisionReason` is shown to Claude in the conversation"; "the conversation stays on the same turn"**; "A timed-out ... hook doesn't block the tool call"; **"All matching hooks run in parallel"**; matcher `a|b` is exact names. **Not given there:** which hook wins when two disagree (only that exit 2 blocks), and the transcript's JSONL schema. I took the schema from the second reader's worker, and from a read-only dry run of my matc

[… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]
28: 2. **What happens to a denied ring.** The seat sees the reason in-turn, as the tool's refusal; the same turn continues; it opens or drops the item and re-sends. Mocked: `IN-TURN` (deny, then Read, then the re-send allowed, ledger `['deny','allow']` with equal ringSha). **Live: see the LIVE section below.**
29: 3. **Gaming, and what I chose (cheap, not airtight).** A Bash/PowerShell command counts toward an item only per SEGMENT (split on `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, newline) whose leading word is not a printer (`echo`, `printf`, `Write-Host/Output/Verbose/Warning/Error/Information`, `true`, `:`) and is not a `#` comment; so `echo path` does not count, `echo x && cat path` does. Also: an item must have COMPLETED without error in this turn (an errored Read, a failed Bash, or a call with no recorded result, such as one issued in the SAME message as the ring, does not count); a Grep over a directory or a Glob counts only if its result names the item. **Not caught:** `node -e "console.log('path')"`, `python -c`, a printer inside a wrapper (`powershell -Command "echo path"`), a Grep with no path whose result merely contains the basename, and any opened file that does not back the claim (the second reader's half).
30: 4. **Turn boundaries.** The turn starts after the seat's last prompt: a user message with text, no tool result, not `isMeta`, not a subagent (sidechain) entry. A tool-result message with a reminder text block riding on it is still a tool result. An item read in a previous turn is denied (`TURN BOUNDARY` test, and the re-read after it is allowed). The transcript is read from the end in a 4 MB window that doubles to 64 MB until a prompt boundary is in view (a 5 MB tool result in the turn is tested).
49: - **`BUILDING.md`** (`consonance/src-tauri/brief/BUILDING.md`, in the commit): WHAT A HAND-BACK OWES gains **item 8, "A SOURCES LINE ON EVERY HAND-OFF RING — and it is a GATE, not a request"**, 19 lines after item 7: the line's format, what the hook denies, that the deny is in-turn and logged first, that an echo does not count, that it fails open, the D210 numbers as the reason (0.979 gated vs 0.010–0.041 ungated), and the one thing it cannot do (whether a file backs the claim). `git show 21be348 -- consonance/src-tauri/brief/BUILDING.md`.

69: - **The transcript-flush race:** that the result of the call made immediately before a ring is already on disk when the hook reads (the model's next turn takes seconds; the live probe below read it, but one probe is not a rate). A lagging flush would deny a ring whose source WAS read: the fix cost is one re-send.

# D197 A — the OpenRouter route for jev-ask — hand-back
Worktree `C:\Users\nname\Desktop\a-d197-wt` (a `git worktree add --detach` of the lighthouse repo at 4ee4200; no junctions; `git status --short` clean after the commit). Sonnet 5.5. INSTRUMENT tier. **Commit (in a-d197-wt only): `07182868d1a31462a96df0b9ddd9dca377a09ba7`**, parent 4ee4200.

## The plan's API shape — checked against OpenRouter's docs before building (no call to the API, only the docs pages)
Sources: openrouter.ai/docs/guides/community/jev.md, …/jev-tutorial.md, and the API reference page for `POST /api/alpha/decisions` (`…/alphadecisions/submit-a-decisions-questions-and-answers-request.md`). **The plan's shape is right; two details it did not spell out, both handled:**
- **OpenRouter's yes/no type is `noul`, not `boolean`, and its answer is `{ type: 'noul', noul: <p> }`.** jev-ask's schema vocabulary is `boolean` | `choice` | `score` and its boolean answer is `{ probability }`. On the openrouter route a `boolean` question is sent as `noul` (criteria `{true, false}` and instructions unchanged) and a `noul` answer is mapped back to `{ type: 'boolean', probability }`; `choice` and `score` pass through. **D162's sealed schema is choice-only (`q3`), so it is untouched by the translation** (tested: its questions go out unchanged).
- Documented response: `{ id, model, provider (string), answers, usage: { input_tokens, output_tokens, cost? } }`; documented error statuses include 400, 401, 402 ("Insufficient credits"), 403, 404, 413, 429, 500, 502, 503, 524, 529. `state` may be a string (jev-ask sends a string).
- I did **not** refuse the plan: the shape matches. Note the docs name `~typesafe/jev-latest` as the alias; the code pins `typesafe/jev-1.13`, as ordered.

## What changed — `consonance/tools/jev-ask.js`
- **Route selection is explicit, never silent.** `ask({ route })` and the CLI `--route vercel|openrouter`. With no route named the route is **vercel, exactly as before** (jev-judge / jev-shadow and their suites speak it, and all 199 of their tests pass untouched). **Nothing switches by which key is present:** an `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` alone with no route named is ref

[… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]
15: - **Response mapping:** `id` → `generationId`; `usage` → `{ inputTokens, outputTokens, cost }`; `cost` as a string (null = NOT REPORTED, never 0); the **resolved** `model` and the `provider` recorded; result gets `route: 'openrouter'` (the Vercel result's keys are unchanged: tested). The ledger row gains `route`.
16: - **A wrong resolved model REFUSES loudly (exit 2, a `Refusal`):** the check runs before anything is read as an answer; the message names the model it saw and the generation id (so the accounts can find the spend) and says the answers are discarded. It requires `typesafe/jev-1.13-<suffix>` (`/^typesafe\/jev-1\.13-[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$/`): the bare pin `typesafe/jev-1.13`, `jev-1.12-…`, `jev-1.130-…`, an alias, another provider, a missing or non-string model are all refused. **A dated 1.13 suffix of any form is accepted** (the next patch release is not a fallback).
17: - **402 / 403 / 429 / 5xx / a non-JSON body / a missing or wrong-typed answer → `GatewayError` (exit 1), no answer invented.**
20: ## Tests (heavy-run lock via `heavy-run.js` hold, `node --max-old-space-size=4096 --test --test-concurrency=1`; no full suite)

22: - `jev-ask.mutants.js`: **50 applied · 50 caught · 0 survived · 0 NOT APPLIED** (control 104/0). New mutants: model an alias; `data_collection` dropped / `allow`; boolean not translated; noul not mapped; resolved-model check removed / loosened to `typesafe/jev-` / downgraded to a GatewayError; recorded model = the one asked for; id, usage tokens, cost not mapped; a missing cost read as 0; default route becomes openrouter; unknown route defaulted; the openrouter route falling back to the other key; the route hint removed; only the route's own key scanned / scrubbed; key shapes not redacted; the dry placeholder naming the wrong variable; the ledger's route dropped. Three existing anchors moved with the code (no-key refusal, key-itself check, scrub) and were updated.

So `mcp__consonance__call_librarian|mcp__consonance__call_chair` is an exact-name matcher (no regex). The stdin payload carries `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`. **Allow:** "Exit 0 with no output or empty JSON" (a deny/ask/exit 2 is the only way to alter a call); **"Other [exit codes]: Non-blocking error. [… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]
12: ## What was built (all under `consonance/`, copied to `~/.claude/shell/hooks/` by `dev/shell/install.ps1`)
13: - **`hooks/second-reader.js`** (the hook). Dream gate; reentrancy exit on `CONSONANCE_SECOND_READER_RUN=1`; acts only on the two tools (`tool_name` must be in the set, else silent exit 0); **returns ALLOW at once: exit 0, no output, ever** (it never prints a decision; no input makes it exit non-zero). One worker at a time: a lock file `<data>/second-reader.lock` taken with `O_EXCL`, `{pid, ts, token}`; a live lock gives a `skipped-busy` row and the ring is not queued; a lock whose pid is dead, or older than 180 s, or unreadable, is stale and replaced. It starts the worker **detached**, hands it the job **over a pipe** (the message text never touches the disk from this hook), and exits in well under two seconds (measured < 1.5 s in the test, with a worker still sleeping for 3.5 s).
14: - **`hooks/second-reader-worker.js`** (the detached half; `Lib = $true` in the manifest). Builds the **turn** from the transcript tail (last 4 MB; the tool calls and results after the seat's last prompt: a user message with text and NO tool result; the ring's own call left out; results clipped, whole bounded at 60,000 characters, oldest results reduced first), asks `claude -p --model claude-sonnet-5-5 --output-format json --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --tools "" --no-session-persistence --disable-slash-commands` with **QS verbatim** (compared against the plan file by a test), prompt on stdin, `cwd` = the temp dir, env `CONSONANCE_SECOND_READER_RUN=1`. **120 s timeout** (kills the child tree with `taskkill /T /F`), plus a watchdog at 130 s, so it cannot outlive itself. **Fails open:** every failure (no transcript, spawn error, non-zero exit, timeout, bad JSON, `is_error`) is ONE `error` row and nothing else; the lock is released in every path.
15: - **The row** (`C:\Consonance\data\second-reader.jsonl`): `{ ts, seat, tool, ringSha (sha256 of the message), status, flags, model, ms, tokens: { in, out, cacheRead, cacheCreate }, costUsd?, chars, turnChars, calls, promptChars, droppedFlags? }`. **The message text is stored by sha only.** *A decision for the chair:* `flags` quote the flagged SENTENCES (that is what a flag is, and B reads them blind), so each stored quote is kept only if it really is a substring of the message (a quote that is not is dropped and counted, never stored: model hallucination guard), at most 300 characters, at most 20 flags, scrubbed. No other part of the message reaches the log (tested: a unique sentence and a path from an unflagged sentence are absent from the log).
16: - **The secret scan** is self-contained in the worker (an installed hook cannot require repo tools): `sk-or-`, `sk-ant-`, `vck_` named, plus the generic `sk-`, GitHub, AWS, Slack, Google, PEM, Bearer, JWT and `NAME=value` shapes. It scrubs what is **sent to the model** (the message and the turn), **what is stored** (flags, error text), and error text from the child. (`sk-or-` and `sk-ant-` are also caught by the generic `sk-…` shape, so removing their named entries alone is an equivalent mutant; the tests assert the tokens are gone, not which entry caught them.)
35: ## Tests (heavy-run lock via `heavy-run.js` hold, `node --max-old-space-size=4096 --test --test-concurrency=1`; no full suite)
36: - **`consonance/hooks/second-reader.test.js`: 29/29** (spawn mocked, data dir a temp dir each time). Covers every item you listed: **the matcher fires on only the two tools** (the registered `Matcher` is parsed from `install.ps1` and checked against the docs' exact-name syntax; nine other tool names, including `chair_inject`, `post_board`, `raise_pull` and a look-alike server, are silent no-ops); **allow is immediate** (< 1.5 s, exit 0, empty stdout, a worker still sleeping, the worker still runs once and finishes: no process left); **errors fail open** (ten malformed inputs, an unwritable and an unresolvable data dir, a missing worker whose dead lock the next ring replaces, plus the worker's own error paths: no transcript, non-zero exit, bad JSON, no flags, spawn throws, spawn error event, timeout); **the lock gives skipped-busy** (live pid; stale by dead pid, by age, and by an unreadable file are replaced; two simultaneous rings start exactly ONE worker); **no message text in the log** (hook rows and worker rows); **the reentrancy guard holds** (the hook exits silently under the variable; the worker it starts carries it; the model child is spawned with `--safe-mode`, `--strict-mcp-config`, no `--mcp-config`, `--tools ""`, no `--bare`, the variable, and a cwd that is not the repo); **the secret scan** scrubs `sk-or-`, `sk-ant-`, `vck_` and the other shapes from the prompt sent, the stored flags, and error rows. ## 2026-09-20 — L061 P-VICSEK-PHI (L, build+measure): a null is only a null for the data it assumes — 1/sqrt(N) assumes directions spread over a sphere, and text embeddings are a cone, so phi read 4.4x its baseline on EVERY session and the ruling's falsifier fired. Built consonance/tools/vicsek-phi.js + .test.js (13/13), importing the encoder and session rule from order-parameter.js / deference-unit.js so there is one copy of each. phi = |mean of unit vectors| is ORDER-INVARIANT and the test asserts it (<1e-12 under a verified shuffle), so its control is a DISTRIBUTION control: 200 draws of N from the board's 14,955 pooled contributions, without replacement, seeded. NULL RE-DERIVED with mulberry32+Box-Muller (uniform on the sphere, not the ruling's LCG): 1/sqrt(N) holds to <=0.5% at d=64 and d=768 out to N=1000 (0.0317 vs 0.0316) where the ruling's generator read 0.046-0.060 and honestly quoted itself only to N=100 — the degradation was the generator, not the statistic. And the exact null is c_d/sqrt(N), c_d = sqrt(2/d)*G((d+1)/2)/G(d/2) = 0.92132 at d=3, 0.99967 at d=768: my d=3 column sits 8% under 1/sqrt(N) on c_3, the ruling's LCG column sits above mine — a fingerprint of non-uniform directions. Registered null untouched: it is right to 0.03% at d=768 where it is applied. BOARD (C's frozen snapshot 2a4a559f, same 118 sessions, join exact 118/118, 846s): median ratio phi/(1/sqrt(N)) 4.386, Q1 3.374, Q3 6.506, max 32.989, 0/118 at or below — prediction WRONG on every session, falsifier CONDITION MET (r-corrected says drift, 36/118), so both readings SUSPENDED per §5. Cause measured: pooled draws read ~0.62 at EVERY N (0.6096-0.6343 across sessions) and do not fall as 1/sqrt(N). Corrected reading vs that null: median ratio 1.065, 91/118 above their own p95, 2 below p05 — real and small, opposite shape to the raw. 2x2 with r: phi-align+r-lock 29, phi-align+r-drift 62, neither 20, r-only 7 — they agree on 49/118 (41.5%). The 2,987-contribution session reads ratio 33 raw and FAILS its pooled control. Named limit, not run: the pool mixes panes and 40 days, so same-pane-same-era draws would cut the 91. W1: I asserted dimension-independence before measuring and my own d=3 test falsified it. W2: the runtime in my first draft said 1,216 s; the log says 846 s embedding, 863 s end to end: `handback/p-vicsek-phi-B_2026-09-20.md`

## 2026-09-16 — L064 P-BLIND-WRITE-READ (L, read-only, 20 min): the hazard in repairing a guard is not that its rows appear too often but that a recovered row claims a time the process never observed — C is CORRECT on both mechanisms, verified at source not from C's quotation: defect 1, the edge is detected inside board_push (main.rs:2046, locked/prev computed at :2050-2051) and nothing polls the lock, so a toggle with no traffic writes no row ("the mute is a property of the lock; the record of it is a property of traffic"); defect 2, BLIND_LAST is AtomicU64::new(0) at :2018 with CLOSED gated on prev==2 at :2065, so a lock removed while the app is down writes no CLOSED, and boundary-check.js:184 (not :175 — that is the doc line) pushes [open, Infinity], so every later window reads UNMEASURED forever. Two citation corrections: the defects are C's §4.1/§4.2, NOT §2 as the dispatch said — C's §2 REFUTES a different numbered claim and C's own :221 points at §4 — so a seat briefed off "§2" may have read the refutation instead of the findings (W1: I read it as C contradicting itself before checking C's index). Q2: more rows is the SAFE direction (more recorded blindness = more UNMEASURED, and UNMEASURED is the guard's safe verdict); the defeat is a span NARROWER than the real window, since blindOverlaps only marks a window UNMEASURED if it overlaps [OPEN,CLOSED]. Persisting BLIND_LAST is safe in its natural form because CLOSED is stamped entry.ts at :2071, necessarily after removal — the edit that flips it is stamping a recovered CLOSED with launch time or a last-known mtime to say "when it really ended", since nothing on disk records when a deleted file was deleted. A poller for defect 1 is dangerous at the OPEN edge unless it back-stamps. The asymmetry that resolves it is already in the code: blind_lock() calls fs::metadata at :2024 and discards it (Ok(_)) — the lock carries its own mtime while it exists, nothing carries a removal time. RULE: back-stamp OPEN from mtime, forward-stamp CLOSED at detection, never the reverse, so every error widens the span. Verdict on E pre-registered before E's file existed (it was not on disk at 07:2x) so it cannot be fitted: DO NOT LAND if any CLOSED carries a time the process did not observe: `handback/p-blind-write-read-B_2026-09-16.md`

BUILD, AMENDED. **All 2 FATAL and 8 AMEND accepted** (AMEND-7 with my change), every number re-derived with its command, and struck wording kept. B's block left byte-identical (313 lines, sha256 `5898223a…`). **FATAL-1 was my own argument turned on me:** I said the stimulus is the unit, then spent the budget on repeats. B's power probe (sha `037c03d3…` verified, re-run, every cell reproduces) shows 30 × 2 beats 12 × 5 at equal calls: an effect on half the stimuli is found 83% of the time against 9%. **FATAL-2 was half the discipline I'm cited for:** I built a sensitivity check (B+) with no specificity check, and an end-of-reply REPORT scored as FOLLOWED on exactly the outcome I predicted most. The fix is a line-of-its-own signature plus a negative control N−. **AMEND-1:** I saw the tag wrapper and inferred its contents. The tagged channel carries pointers, the body arrives by Read, so C vs D is now descriptive. **AMEND-5:** I had turned B's labelled ESTIMATE into "re-derived" by redoing its arithmetic. The measured per-call overhead is 13,215 tokens, so the cost is 10.28M, not 5.83M, and the stop rule now sums all three `modelUsage` fields.
Added by me: a CP floor under B's bootstrap for D's absolute rate (an all-zero bootstrap bound is 0 exactly when data is thinnest). The pointer route now holds at 0–1 follows per 60 and fails at 2. I also caught two errors B didn't name: I had called an undercount "conservative for every claim", which is anti-conservative for "D is low"; and arm A runs on Opus 5 at twice 5.5's time. NOTE-7 ruled: the six B-authored and six E-authored stimuli don't matter to subjects, and the absolute rate is also printed without them.
**Carry:** (1) If you say the unit is X, spend the budget on more X. (2) For every property a scorer claims, one positive and one negative control. (3) Redoing an estimate's arithmetic does not make it a measurement, so say which one you have.

## 2026-09-20 — D092 P-ASK-PROVENANCE (D, build): a correction that cannot reach the queue because its only channel is the one its author must not use is a ROUTING problem, and the fix is a READER not a writer — a goal's re-test now renders beside its ask with date and path:line while the store stays untouched. ask.js still never writes (:26); it reads the goals' own files at render time, which makes "reaches the queue the day it is written" true by construction. Three forms: (A) BLOCK, an ASK-id inside a paragraph matching /open asks re-tested/i — the shape daily-news-digest already writes, so its EXISTING output is carried unchanged; (B) MARKER, one line `RE-TESTED ASK-0NN <date>: <result>` in any live goal file, the forward channel, undated = REFUSED not dated by the reader; (C) the Source-line convention `Re-tested <date>: <result>, per <path>:<line>` for a seat freezing it, per E's D091 ASK-006 precedent — never Question, never Status. Live files only: /evidence/, -versions/, .pre-, .bak excluded, because the one block exists in 8 archived copies plus a .pre-P71 sibling. VARIABILITY CHECK FIRST: 4 of 12 open asks carry one, 8 do not — not fixed at 0 or at all. NULL registered beside the falsifier (10:36:51, sha256 11939515) and PASSED: 0 attachments across PENDING-CONDITIONS.md (7 mentions), progress.md (13, incl :1322 "ASK-004/005/002/013 re-tested" in prose), STANDING-ITEMS.md:144; the 4 attached are exactly the packet's, with the goal's own ages 38/34/9/5. DEFECT IN MY OWN READER, found by running it live not on fixtures: it dated the block 2026-09-01 from a <lastmod> quoted two sentences away when the pass entry is 2026-09-03 — two days stale; a mis-dated re-test is worse than an undated one because an undated one is refused and a mis-dated one is believed. Fixed: the date must OPEN its line; no pass entry = undated, said out loud. Red first (7 tests failed on TypeError before the reader existed), 41/41 plain, --test, --test-concurrency=4, filtered 4/4; mutants 11 applied / 11 caught / 0 survived / 0 NOT APPLIED, control GREEN. W: mutant run 1 claimed 10/10 with a RED control (copy tree lacked exo_memory/ASK.md) and 5 anchors mangled by heredoc backslashes — FOURTH lap running; run 2 caught 5/11 and all 6 survivors were real test gaps (archive fixture failed on extension not the archive rule; null fixture ids not bolded; M3 and M11 equivalent as written). NOT verified: form (B) has never been emitted by a goal; the only live block is 17 days old and later passes head condition 6 differently, so (A) can be missed SILENTLY and there is no instrument for that: `handback/p-ask-provenance-B_2026-09-20.md`
